# Claims

The authoritative list. Nothing in this repository is a claim unless it appears
here with an evidence pointer that resolves. `scripts/check_claims.py` verifies
every row and fails CI if a pointer is dead.

## Format

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| ID | `C-NNN`, never reused, never renumbered. |
| Statement | What is asserted, precisely enough to be wrong. Name the dataset, the split, the seed and the metric. |
| Setting | Dataset, split type, model set. A number without its setting is not a claim. |
| Evidence | `experiments/<dir>`, produced by `scripts/record_run.py`. |
| Status | `active` or `superseded by C-NNN`. |

A claim is never edited in place once it has a resolving pointer. To correct
one, mark it superseded and add a new row.

A statement about the *literature* — what a paper did or did not measure — is
not a claim in this sense and does not belong here. It belongs in
[../landscape/prior-art.md](../landscape/prior-art.md) with a `verified` status
and a section pointer, because its evidence is a document rather than a run.

The Yurdakul–Naranjo test of `psi-inference` 0.1.0 rejects in 0.049–0.054 (10 bins) and 0.049–0.063 (20 bins), and in 0.049–0.052 at 5,000 or more rows per side. | C-003 | The origination axis of the Lending Club accepted-loans file `accepted_2007_to_2018Q4.csv.gz` runs from 2007-06 to 2018-12 over 47 quarterly cohorts. Of the eleven columns the run parses as dates, none carries a value later than 2019-06-01, which stands in `hardship_end_date` and is 99.517% null. Eight of them reach past the last origination — `last_pymnt_d`, `next_pymnt_d`, `last_credit_pull_d`, `hardship_start_date`, `hardship_end_date`, `payment_plan_start_date`, `debt_settlement_flag_date`, `settlement_date` — so each carries values dated after the book stopped originating, and none of them can define an origination-time split. The file holds 2,260,668 loans and 33 rows carrying no origination date. Under the five terminal statuses of the label module 1,348,099 loans are resolved, 59.63%; the 26 cohorts from 2007Q2 to 2013Q3 are resolved in full, 31 cohorts carry at least 5,000 resolved loans, and the resolved share falls from 88.04% at 2015Q4 to 83.60% at 2016Q1, 48.43% at 2017Q1, 20.89% at 2018Q1 and 3.92% at 2018Q4. | Two passes over the file published as `wordsforthewise/lending-club` on Kaggle, 392,582,231 bytes, sha256 `55c16f75120f897683f02e7aabcf080d0e4a20c4832feb1d592cfa941bd62a2d`. The date columns are found rather than named: one row in every hundred, spread across the file because it is a concatenation of quarterly releases, and every string column of that sample whose non-null values parse as `%b-%Y` above 95% is kept. Origination is `issue_d`. No split, no model, no seed: the reading is a function of the file. | experiments/2026-09-19-lc-vintage-structure | active |
| C-004 | Charge-off within N months of origination, at the 2019-03-01 snapshot the file itself reports and a five-month charge-off lag, labels 1,873,290 loans over 44 quarterly cohorts at 2.8989% for N = 12 and 1,418,626 over 40 cohorts at 10.2747% for N = 24. Over the cohorts carrying at least 5,000 labelled loans and 100 defaults, the twelve-month rate runs from 1.919% [1.795, 2.052] at 2013Q4 to 3.444% [3.331, 3.560] at 2016Q2, a factor of 1.79, and the twenty-four-month rate from 7.912% [7.663, 8.168] at 2013Q4 to 11.888% [11.688, 12.091] at 2016Q3, a factor of 1.50; the Wilson intervals of the two extremes are disjoint in both windows, and the extremes are the range of a trajectory rather than a tested change point. A bad loan with no last payment on record is dated at origination plus the lag: 1,937 loans for N = 12 and 1,297 for N = 24. Wilson intervals at 95%, with no adjustment for the selection of the extremes. No model, no seed. | experiments/2026-09-19-lc-label-trajectory | active |
| C-005 | On the 28 twelve-month cohorts of at least 5,000 labelled loans, the choice of charge-off lag moves the level far more than the shape, and neither is negligible: the unweighted mean of the cohort default rates falls from 5.685% at a lag of zero months to 3.890% at three, 2.751% at five and 0.242% at eleven, while the Spearman correlation of the cohort trajectory against the five-month one is 0.754 at zero months, 0.929 at three, 0.868 at eight and 0.653 at eleven. Five months is the shortest lag in the sweep leaving no labelled loan still running at the snapshot with a last payment early enough for the lag to place its estimated default inside the window: 34,014 such loans at zero months, 495 at three, none at five. Direct standardising to the 2015Q2 grade mix lifts the twelve-month rate from 3.444% to 3.943% at 2016Q2 and from 3.139% to 3.982% at 2018Q1, the E-to-G share of the cohort falling from 12.368% at 2015Q2 to 4.162% at 2018Q1. | The same file, snapshot and label as C-004, N = 12 throughout; lags 0, 2, 3, 5, 8 and 11 months. The Spearman correlation ranks rates rounded to five decimals, with average ranks for ties. | C-006 | On the expanding arm of the Lending Club grid, the slope of AUC on model age in quarters with one intercept per build is no worse for either foundation model than for the gradient-boosted control that sees the identical 50,000 context rows: paired over the 99 criterion cells, TabICL − GBM-50k is +0.00055 [+0.00020, +0.00088] and TabPFN − GBM-50k +0.00041 [+0.00007, +0.00075], both excluding zero under the primary bootstrap seed and both check seeds and on each of the three context draws held fixed. With the cohorts resampled the two read +0.00055 [−0.00020, +0.00107] and +0.00041 [−0.00032, +0.00091], holding zero — TabICL's resampled interval excludes zero under one check seed ([+0.00003, +0.00109], 20260907) and holds zero under the other two, so it holds zero at the margin. H1's kill does not fire, and its second branch does not fire either: the difference of slopes leaves its interval for both foundation models against the control. With every build weighted alike, the pooling both pre-registrations require beside the arm's, the two differences hold zero: TabPFN − GBM-50k is −0.00002 [−0.00090, +0.00107] and TabICL − GBM-50k +0.00031 [−0.00050, +0.00125]. TabPFN's equal-weight reading is opposite in sign to its arm reading, so by the note of 2026-09-12 its difference is not read as a property of the arm; TabICL's agrees in sign. No model's own slope excludes zero under this identification (scorecard −0.00046 [−0.00150, +0.00064], GBM −0.00022 [−0.00128, +0.00083], GBM-50k −0.00033 [−0.00137, +0.00074], TabPFN +0.00008 [−0.00093, +0.00132], TabICL +0.00022 [−0.00073, +0.00135]), while under the cohort identification every model's own slope lies below zero beyond its interval (scorecard −0.00282 [−0.00319, −0.00244], GBM-50k −0.00181 [−0.00231, −0.00122], TabPFN −0.00196 [−0.00242, −0.00151], TabICL −0.00267 [−0.00306, −0.00225]); no own slope is read as ageing under either. On the second identification — one intercept per cohort, added on 2026-09-12 and named as an addition — the two models part: TabPFN − GBM-50k is −0.00016 [−0.00046, +0.00008], holding zero under every seed with the cohorts fixed and resampled alike and starred on one of the three draws (20260912, −0.00027 [−0.00055, −0.00003]), while TabICL − GBM-50k is −0.00087 [−0.00136, −0.00051], excluding zero below zero under every seed, with the cohorts resampled, and on every draw. For TabPFN the row therefore supports that its slope is no worse than the control's under both identifications, and no more: its arm-level star is not read as a property of the arm, the equal-weight pooling disagreeing in sign; for TabICL it supports only the weaker claim: no worse under the criterion's identification, and worse beyond the interval under the cohort identification, the identification on which the two disagree, and the disagreement runs against TabICL. The statement is a comparison of two models on identical cells and not a statement about a model class. | Lending Club `accepted_2007_to_2018Q4.csv.gz`, the file and origination column of C-003, twelve-month charge-off label at a five-month lag as C-004 defines it, immature loans dropped. Expanding arm, nine as-of dates at the end of each half-year from 2013-06 to 2017-06; builds 2013H1-E to 2017H1-E holding 19, 17, 15, 13, 11, 9, 7, 5 and 3 criterion cells, 99 in all over 19 distinct quarterly cohorts, so 2013H1-E carries 19 of the 99 and the arm's pooling is cell-weighted for the level metrics while this slope weights each build by the spread of its ages; 33,660,000 scored rows over 380,000 distinct loans, 20,000 rows per test quarter under seed 20260902. Blind gap `(T − 12m, T]`: rows originated inside it are neither trained on nor scored. Models: scorecard (monotonic WOE, IV ≥ 0.02, at most 20 characteristics, logistic regression), LightGBM on the full pool, LightGBM on the same 50,000-row context the foundation models read and inheriting its build's full-pool hyperparameters, TabPFN-3 and TabICLv2 at defaults with no fine-tuning. Foundation-model temperature 0.9, the shipped setting, with 1.0 beside; the temperature is a monotone transform of the score, and the run records TabICL's 1.0 rows as reading zero difference in this slope (`tabicl@t1 - tabicl` = 0.0 exactly), so H1 does not depend on it. The control's tuning budget is a fixed small grid with time-ordered inner validation; the foundation models get none, and the asymmetry is named here. | C-007 | On the expanding arm of the Lending Club grid, at the shipped temperature of 0.9, TabICL's mean absolute Cox-slope deviation exceeds the full-pool scorecard's beyond the interval, paired over the 99 criterion cells: +0.0473 [+0.0116, +0.0681], excluding zero under the primary bootstrap seed and both check seeds and with the cohorts resampled under all three, so H2's kill fires for TabICL at 0.9. At 1.0 neither exceeds the scorecard: TabICL −0.0055 [−0.0159, +0.0112] and TabPFN −0.0045 [−0.0169, +0.0122], holding zero under every seed with the cohorts fixed and resampled alike. The verdict differs between the two settings and is reported as differing. Against the control on identical rows the same statistic holds zero at 0.9 (TabICL −0.0061 [−0.0746, +0.0479], TabPFN −0.0325 [−0.0983, +0.0207]) and lies below zero at 1.0 (−0.0589 [−0.1101, −0.0144] and −0.0579 [−0.1106, −0.0212]), excluding zero under every seed and both cohort settings. The row-count asymmetry between the scorecard, fitted on the whole pool, and the foundation models, fitted on 50,000 rows, is measured on the same statistic by the control's own row against the scorecard: +0.0534 [+0.0187, +0.1009], excluding zero under every seed and with the cohorts resampled. The shipped setting is a fixed scale on the logit and moves both models further from one by arithmetic rather than by anything the book did: the arm's mean signed Cox slope is 0.9605 for TabICL at 1.0 against 0.8645 at 0.9 and 1.0011 for TabPFN at 1.0 against 0.9010 at 0.9, the scorecard's is 0.9818 and the control's 1.0851, so a slope at or just below one is multiplied by 0.9 exactly. TabICL's 1.0 rows are derived from its 0.9 scores by that scale, exactly, checked on 70,000 rows of each build's own cells, where the largest absolute probability gap over the nine builds is 1.6 × 10⁻⁷; TabPFN's rows at both settings are separately scored, and its slope at the shipped setting reads 0.90096 against the 0.90097 the scale predicts. On this statistic the run's `draws_disagree` index names six pairs — each of the four foundation-model rows against the control, and the control against the scorecard and against the full-pool GBM — and neither model's row against the scorecard, the criterion's own comparison, is among them; the index fires when two of a pair's three per-draw intervals are disjoint, the per-draw poolings being at the arm's scope alone. No sentence here is about a model class. | As C-006's setting, with these additions read from the same run. The criterion statistic is the mean over the cells in scope of the absolute deviation of the Cox slope from one, paired by cell, the signed slope pooled over the same estimable cells; a cell on which some model's Cox fit did not finish is not estimable and leaves the pairing for every model at every scope. The criterion's comparator is the full-pool scorecard, as the amendment of 2026-09-04 rewrote H2; the control on identical rows is reported beside it. Both temperatures are reported, 0.9 as the criterion's and 1.0 beside. | C-014 | The origination axis of the Freddie Mac Single-Family Loan-Level Dataset sample is the year and quarter encoded in the loan identifier, and the dataset carries no origination date. It runs from 1999Q1 to 2026Q1 over 109 quarterly cohorts holding 1,362,500 loans, 12,500 in each, the exceptions being 2008Q4 at 12,499 and 2009Q1 at 12,501. The origination file carries two dates, the first payment date and the maturity date, and only the first can run a clock from origination. It agrees with that axis. The lag behind the opening of the origination quarter has a median of 3 months in every one of the 109 cohorts and a 95th percentile of 3 to 5 months, and no loan anywhere in the book has a first payment before its quarter opens. The lag reaches six months or more on 0.521% of the book, and in no cohort on more than 4.576%, the share in 2000Q1. Those 7,095 loans are the ones the loader reads as re-dated, on that threshold alone; what the run measures is the lag and not the nature of the date behind it. The exclusion is not idle: 9 of the re-dated loans reach ninety days past due or real-estate-owned acquisition before age one, against none of the 1,355,405 that are not re-dated, of which 977 terminate before age one instead. Ten loans have no performance record at all and are never labelled. | The sample files of the Single-Family Loan-Level Dataset, Release 47 of July 2026, `sample_1999.zip` through `sample_2026.zip`, published with an origination and a performance cutoff of 2026-03-31 and holding a random 50,000 loans for each full origination year and fewer for the partial one, 2026. The reduction that reads them is recorded separately as `experiments/2026-09-06-fm-reduce3`: it asserts the hand-transcribed Release 47 layout against every zip by column count, 31 in the origination file and 35 in the performance file, hashes all 28, and writes one row per loan and the first twenty-four months of every loan, filing each loan under the quarter its identifier names. The run cited here reads those reduced files and records the hash of all 28 of them in its own summary. The clock is the first payment date: a loan is one month old in its first payment month. `LATE_FIRST_PAYMENT_MONTHS` states the six-month threshold at which a loan is read as re-dated, and the reason for it. No split, no model, no seed: the reading is a function of the files. | experiments/2026-09-19-fm-vintage-structure | active |
| C-015 | On the Freddie Mac Single-Family Loan-Level Dataset sample, a default is read as the first month at or over ninety days past due, or a month of real-estate-owned acquisition, or a termination by third-party sale, short sale or charge-off, or real-estate-owned disposition, at or before the window closes; a month counts whether or not the loan is under a borrower assistance plan or a declared disaster delinquency. Read that way over twelve months from the first payment month, and after 7,095 re-dated loans and 10 with no performance record are excluded, the sample labels 1,298,994 loans with 6,697 defaults, a rate of 0.005156, and drops 54,931 as immature and 1,470 as censored. Over twenty-four months it labels 1,248,788 with 18,165 defaults, a rate of 0.014546, and drops 104,929 as immature and 1,678 as censored. Maturity is judged per loan rather than per cohort, so the latest cohort carrying a label is labelled in part: 2025Q1 on 7,518 of its 12,500 loans at twelve months, and 2024Q1 on 7,445 at twenty-four. Against floors of 5,000 labelled loans and 100 defaults the twelve-month window clears 11 of the 109 quarterly cohorts, 23 half-years and 21 of the 27 years that carry a label, and the twenty-four-month window clears 55, 48 and 26, which is every year that carries one, the last of them being 2024 on the 7,445 loans just named. The eleven quarterly cohorts are 2007Q3 through 2008Q4 and 2019Q2 through 2020Q2. A quarterly twelve-month trajectory on this sample is therefore a trajectory of those two stretches and of nothing between them. Over the cohorts that clear the floors the twelve-month rate runs from 0.00835 on 2020Q2, whose interval is 0.00689 to 0.0101, to 0.03778 on 2019Q4, 0.03457 to 0.04127; the twenty-four-month rate from 0.0081 on 2010Q2, 0.00667 to 0.00983, to 0.07045 on 2007Q4, 0.06608 to 0.07509. Those are the ends of a range and not a change point, and 2010Q2 holds its end by one loan, clearing the default floor with 101. | The label module reads the reduced performance slice of `experiments/2026-09-06-fm-reduce3`. A loan observed through the window with none of the events above is a non-default, a loan paid off inside the window is a non-default, a loan that leaves the data inside the window through a whole-loan sale, a reperforming-loan sale or a confirmed defect is censored and dropped, and a loan whose record ends inside the window with no termination is immature and dropped. The label keys its performance cutoff to the month the file ends, 2026-03-01, which is the last month of the published cutoff of 2026-03-31, and a loan whose window has not closed by then is dropped as immature whatever its record says. The intervals are Wilson intervals at 95%. The floors are the ones the first book's trajectory uses. The recorded label definition names neither the truncation parameter nor the gap exclusion that the modelled grid sets, so a loan whose servicing record begins after its window opens is labelled here on the part that was observed, and the counts are the sample's and not the grid's. No split, no model, no seed. | experiments/2026-09-19-fm-vintage-structure | active |
| C-016 | On the Freddie Mac Single-Family Loan-Level Dataset sample, a month a loan spends under a borrower assistance plan or a declared disaster delinquency can be counted towards default as the reported status does, or set aside; the choice moves the level of the book, the number of cohorts it can be read on, and at twelve months which cohort reads as its worst. Over twelve months the defaults fall from 6,697 of 1,298,994 labelled loans to 4,555 of 1,298,980, a rate of 0.005156 against 0.003507, and over twenty-four months from 18,165 of 1,248,788 to 13,630 of 1,248,757, a rate of 0.014546 against 0.010915. The labelled counts differ by 14 and 31 because that many loans are censored under the set-aside reading instead of defaulting, 1,470 against 1,484 and 1,678 against 1,709, while the immature counts do not move. The cohorts clearing floors of 5,000 labelled loans and 100 defaults fall too: at twelve months from 11 quarterly cohorts, 23 half-years and 21 years to 6, 16 and 19, and at twenty-four months from 55, 48 and 26 to 41, 45 and 26. The six that survive at twelve months are 2007Q3 through 2008Q4. The five cohorts from 2019Q2 to 2020Q2 therefore clear the floors on a quarterly twelve-month reading only when relief counts, and the worst quarterly cohort changes with them: 2019Q4 at 0.03778 when relief counts, and 2007Q4 at 0.01904 when it is set aside, that being 2007Q4's rate under both. At twenty-four months nothing of the kind happens to the worst cohort, which is 2007Q4 at 0.07045 under either reading. The difference between the readings is concentrated rather than general. Of a year's reported defaults, the share that is not a default once relief is set aside reaches 0.40 in five origination years at twelve months and in no others — 0.913 in 2019, 0.8624 in 2020, 0.6162 in 2021, 0.4577 in 2022 and 0.4474 in 2017, against 0.2973 for the next highest, 2016 — and in four at twenty-four months, 0.8926 in 2019, 0.7732 in 2020, 0.7678 in 2018 and 0.5044 in 2021, against 0.2774 for 2016. It also begins at one point on the axis and nowhere before it. Every labelled quarterly cohort from 1999Q1 to 2013Q1 reads the same under both readings at twelve months, 57 of them, and every one from 1999Q1 to 2012Q1 does at twenty-four, 53 of them. After each of those boundaries 48 labelled cohorts remain under each window, of which 46 differ and two agree: 2013Q3 and 2015Q2 at twelve months, 2012Q3 and 2013Q1 at twenty-four. The twenty-four-month rate range over the cohorts that clear the floors is unchanged by the choice, 0.0081 on 2010Q2 to 0.07045 on 2007Q4. | `experiments/2026-09-19-fm-vintage-structure` labels the same loans twice. The reduction it reads, `experiments/2026-09-06-fm-reduce3`, records two ages for every loan — the first month reaching the default condition, and the first reaching it in a month carrying neither flag — and the label module reads one or the other; its `relief_months_count` records which was passed. The two readings therefore differ in that column and in nothing else, and are produced side by side in one pass. The floors are the ones the first book's trajectory uses, and the six quarterly cohorts the set-aside reading leaves at twelve months are below the eight that EXP-003's first kill criterion asks for. ADR-0006 does not rest the reading on that count: it sets relief aside because a legislated payment holiday is not a credit event, and reads the book at twenty-four months on half-year cohorts because that is the finest resolution the floors allow. No split, no model, no seed. | experiments/2026-09-19-fm-vintage-structure | active |

| C-028 | On the rolling arm of the Freddie Mac grid, scored on the rows of its own context at the shipped temperature of 0.9, each foundation model's observed-over-expected ratio depends on that context's realised default rate beyond the interval: the highest-rate context minus the lowest, 2010H2-R at a training-pool rate of 4.4822% against 2018H2-R at 0.4496%, is −0.355 [−0.581, −0.175] for TabPFN and −0.632 [−0.897, −0.405] for TabICL, both excluding zero below zero under the primary bootstrap seed and both check seeds, the nearest check-seed bound −0.140 and −0.360; the ratios themselves are 1.246 against 1.601 and 1.098 against 1.731, the shortfall larger where the rate is lower, and H5's kill fires on the rolling arm at 0.9 as the criterion reads it on the ratio matrix, the criterion's; on the nominal matrix, the ablation reported beside every verdict of this book since the `upb_nominal` trigger fired, the reading is the same, −0.354 [−0.578, −0.177] for TabPFN and −0.643 [−0.918, −0.405] for TabICL, both excluding zero below zero under all three seeds, the nearest check-seed bound −0.137 and −0.362, the ratios 1.246 against 1.600 and 1.102 against 1.745, so the kill fires there too and the nominal reading changes nothing. What the check does not cover is named with it: no row of a rolling build is compared with a scored row, and the contexts the error is measured on span a narrower range of rates than the ones it is carried to. On the nominal matrix TabPFN's rows at 1.0 are derived on both arms, since that grid scored nothing at 1.0, and the check of every build of both arms is the one check that matrix allows, the four cells of 2004H2-E the falsifying run scored at 1.0, 121,595 rows: the worst cell error is 0.000177 with a mean of 0.000161, none of the four is above the Lending Club figure of 0.00084, and the observed-over-expected gap on the checked cells is at worst −0.0010; the contexts the nominal rows at 1.0 are carried to are checked on no cell of their own, and the one checked build's rate lies between theirs. TabICL's nominal rows at 1.0 are the same exact scale, checked on the same 121,595 rows with a largest gap of 1.3 × 10⁻⁷. Read per context draw from the run's `cells.csv`, at 1.0 TabPFN's ratio is the higher on the higher-rate context of both arms on every draw — 0.995441, 0.995591 and 1.005408 against 0.979141, 0.979450 and 0.976732 on the expanding arm, 1.008065, 1.005829 and 1.008175 against 0.973366, 0.977623 and 0.969848 on the rolling arm — while TabICL's is the lower on the rolling arm on every draw, 0.968051, 0.920576 and 0.800665 against 1.051676, 1.039602 and 1.013733, so neither model's sign at 1.0 turns on which draw is read. A kill at 0.9 is therefore not read as evidence of a prior. The reading at 1.0 was fixed beside the criterion before any number existed; that it is where a scale and a prior would separate is the reading of the note of 2026-09-17, dated after this result. | C-029 | On the Lending Club accepted-loans book the builds load, 2,260,668 loans carrying an origination date, 1,862,841 of them originated in the window, 107 columns are knowable at origination. 20 enter the matrix every model reads and 87 do not: 4 are out before anything is measured, 6 as redundant, 68 for coverage and 9 by the value rule. The 4 are three free-text fields, `emp_title`, `title`, `desc`, with `zip_code`, geography below the state. Of the 6, the identity each is dropped on is measured over the whole book and not the window, rather than assumed. Four duplicate a kept column: `funded_amnt` equals `loan_amnt` on 99.91% of the loans, `funded_amnt_inv` on 93.30% with a correlation of 0.9990, `fico_range_high` sits 4 or 5 points above `fico_range_low` and at the modal offset on 99.98%, and `grade` is the first letter of `sub_grade` on 100%. Two are functions of another column and the calendar. `int_rate` equals the modal rate of its origination month and sub-grade on 94.66% of the loans, so given `sub_grade` its residual is mostly the month the loan was written; `installment` lies within five cents of the level-payment amortisation of `loan_amnt` at `int_rate` over `term` on 99.86%, with a median gap of 0.005. Either column would let a model date a loan, so both are out for every model. The 68 fall in six onset batches: 10 columns first reach half their peak coverage in 2012Q2, 2 in 2012Q3, 25 in 2012Q4, 1 in 2013Q2, 14 in 2016Q1 and 16 in 2017Q3, and on every one of the 68 the measured onset, read from the run's record of the column beside the onset it was declared at, equals the declared one; the gate itself tests only that the column is late, not the quarter. On every one of them but `mths_since_last_record` the first cohort's share is zero at the four places the run records; that one is carried by 6.72% of the first cohort against a peak quarterly share of 20.36%, a third of the peak against the floor of 0.5, and reaches half that peak in 2013Q2. The 9 carry the calendar in their values rather than in their presence: eight of them hold a single value or level on every first-cohort loan carrying one, `addr_state` holds one of 42 states there, and each is out for a value the first cohort did not hold; they are the subject of C-030. Of the 20 kept, three are unordered categoricals, `home_ownership`, `purpose`, `verification_status`; one, `credit_history_months`, is derived from `earliest_cr_line` as months to origination; and on no kept column, after the two clips of C-031, does the share of loans carrying a value or level the first cohort did not hold exceed 0.69%, the share on `pub_rec_bankruptcies`. | The accepted-loans file of C-003, sha256 `55c16f75120f897683f02e7aabcf080d0e4a20c4832feb1d592cfa941bd62a2d`, read as the Lending Club builds read it: every loan whose `issue_d` parses, with the columns read by the matrix, by the label or by the gates, nothing else. The knowable columns are those `lending_club.FEATURE_AVAILABILITY` lists at an offset of zero or less from origination; a kept derived column stands for the raw column it is computed from, so the partition is over raw columns. The three reports of `features` run as the builds call them before fitting anything, on the same book: the redundancy report, which measures the four duplicate identities and the two calendar-carrier identities over the whole book and not the window; the coverage report, on the window 2010Q2 to 2018Q1, which reads for each column the share of loans carrying a value per origination quarter and calls a column present from the start when its share in 2010Q2 is at least half its peak share (`COVERAGE_FLOOR` 0.5), the onset being the first quarter reaching that half; and the value report of C-030 and C-031, at a value floor of 0.05. `int_rate`'s agreement is the share of loans whose rate equals the modal rate of their origination-month-and-sub-grade cell; `installment`'s is the share within 0.05 of the amortisation formula. The gates refuse to run on a book where a declared list disagrees with what is measured, and this book raised no disagreement. The first cohort of 2010Q2, the coverage rule by value and the two clips are those of EXP-002's amendment of 2026-09-04, late, which the gates implement as written. No model, no split, no seed: the reading is a function of the file and of the gates' constants. | experiments/2026-09-23-lc-features | active |
| C-030 | Nine columns of the Lending Club book date a loan through a value or a level the first cohort did not hold: eight of them carry a single value or level on every loan of the first cohort carrying one, `addr_state` carries one of 42 states there, and all nine are out of the matrix for every model. Five are counts that read zero on every loan of the first cohort carrying a value and take their first non-zero value in 2012Q4, which the pre-registration reads as a back-fill with zeros where the field was not yet collected; the run measures the values and not their origin. Over the window `tax_liens` is non-zero on 3.43% of the loans carrying a value, the twelve-month collections count on 1.67%, the twelve-month charge-off count on 0.79%, `acc_now_delinq` on 0.47% and `delinq_amnt` on 0.38%. Three categoricals hold one level in the first cohort and gain a second later: `initial_list_status` is `f` on every loan carrying a value until the whole-loan programme of 2012Q4 and the other level then covers 64.25% of the window's loans carrying a value; `application_type` is `Individual` until the joint applications of 2015Q4, which cover 3.65%; `disbursement_method` is `Cash` until 2016Q1, the other level covering 0.75%. `addr_state` does the same by degrees: the first cohort holds 42 states, and a state outside them holds 8.25% of the window's loans carrying a state, the first of them written in 2010Q4. Only `initial_list_status` and `addr_state` exceed the value floor of 5%; the other seven are out because they are constant in the first cohort and vary afterwards, whatever their share, the largest of those shares being `application_type`'s 3.65%. On a training pool that straddles the onset, a non-zero count, the second level or a late state is "originated after", and a model with no bin-size floor can split on it; the rule removes the column rather than the value because the level is the calendar. | As C-029: the same book, the same window, the same first cohort; the value report of `features` run as the builds call it; the first cohort and the value rule are those of EXP-002's amendment of 2026-09-04, late. The two twelve-month counts are `collections_12_mths_ex_med` and `chargeoff_within_12_mths`. For each of the nine, measured raw, the report records the number of distinct values or levels in the first cohort; the share of loans carrying a value outside that support, over the loans of the window carrying any value, where the support is the set of levels the first cohort held for a categorical, the single value for a column constant there, or the minimum and maximum for a numeric column; and the onset, the first origination quarter holding such a value. A column is a value carrier if it is constant in the first cohort and varies later, or if its share exceeds `VALUE_FLOOR`, 0.05; every kept column is measured the same way after its transforms and clips and must fail both tests, and every column `features.VALUE_CARRIERS` lists must satisfy one, or the run stops. The reasons quoted are the ones that module records against each column and the run re-measures. No model, no split, no seed. | experiments/2026-09-23-lc-features | active |
| C-031 | Two kept numeric columns of the Lending Club book carry the calendar in their range rather than their support, and are clipped to the first cohort's own bounds before any model reads them: `dti` to 0 to 24.99 and `loan_amnt` to 1,000 to 25,000, the declared bounds equal to the measured minimum and maximum of 2010Q2 on both. Measured before the clip, over the loans of the window carrying a value, a value outside those bounds sits on 23.21% of the loans for `dti` and 13.09% for `loan_amnt`, and every one of them is pinned to the bound. Neither cap pins a loan in 2010Q2, 2010Q3 or 2010Q4; both first pin loans in 2011Q1, 2.69% of that quarter's loans on `dti` and 3.54% on `loan_amnt`. The `dti` share steps to 18.96% in 2012Q3, peaks at 27.36% in 2016Q1 and stands at 25.07% in 2018Q1; the `loan_amnt` share reaches 10.04% in 2012Q4 and peaks in the last quarter of the window, 2018Q1, at 17.91%. After the clip no value of either column lies outside its first-cohort bounds; the share the clip pins — zero in 2010Q2 to 2010Q4, between 2.69% and 6.94% of a quarter's loans from 2011Q1 to 2012Q2 and between 16.32% and 27.36% from 2012Q3 for `dti`, between 3.54% and 17.91% from 2011Q1 for `loan_amnt` — sits at the bound and remains a level a model can split on, so the clip adds the pinned share to whatever mass the first cohort held at the bound rather than removing the calendar, and the support test of C-030 cannot see a mass at a value the first cohort held. The first cohort's own share at each bound is not recorded by the run. | As C-029: the same book, the same window, the same first cohort; the value report of `features` run as the builds call it; the first cohort and the clip are those of EXP-002's amendment of 2026-09-04, late. The bounds are `features.CLIPPED`; the report measures the first cohort's minimum and maximum of each column and stops on a disagreement with the declared bounds. The share pinned is the share of loans carrying a value that lies below the lower bound or above the upper one, measured on the raw column before the clip, over the window as a whole; the same share per origination quarter is recorded beside it, and after the clip that share is zero by construction. The run does not record the share of the first cohort's own loans at either bound, nor any model's use of the bound. No model, no split, no seed. | experiments/2026-09-23-lc-features | active |
| C-032 | On 2015H1-E, the falsifying build of the Lending Club grid, the published protocol — five stratified random folds of the build's training pool — is read beside this one on the five models, and EXP-004's three kill criteria and its branch kill are read as registered and under the rules the criteria script fixes: none fires. Criterion 1, that the protocol does not change the ranking, does not fire. Of the seven Gini pairs starred out of time, six have the same sign in time with a fold spread clear of zero on that side — GBM − scorecard +0.0197 [+0.0139, +0.0251] out of time and +0.0306 [+0.0089, +0.0470] in time, TabPFN − scorecard +0.0233 [+0.0168, +0.0317] and +0.0284 [+0.0153, +0.0475], TabICL − scorecard +0.0270 [+0.0207, +0.0331] and +0.0233 [+0.0099, +0.0374], GBM-50k − GBM −0.0128 [−0.0222, −0.0044] and −0.0290 [−0.0468, −0.0022], TabPFN − GBM-50k +0.0164 [+0.0066, +0.0314] and +0.0268 [+0.0141, +0.0474], TabICL − GBM-50k +0.0201 [+0.0113, +0.0306] and +0.0217 [+0.0065, +0.0387] — and the seventh is TabICL − GBM, +0.0073 [+0.0011, +0.0129] out of time and −0.0073 [−0.0244, +0.0147] in time: random folds of the pool put the GBM ahead of TabICL and cannot separate them, and the cohorts that follow the pool put TabICL ahead beyond the interval in the pooling the criterion reads, all eleven cohorts held fixed under the primary seed, where the star holds under both check seeds as well ([+0.0011, +0.0126] and [+0.0011, +0.0131]). Of the three cohort poolings under the primary seed with no draw held fixed, it is the only one starred; with a draw held fixed the pair is starred on two draws of three. With the cohorts resampled the same difference is +0.0073 [−0.0012, +0.0147] and holds zero under the primary seed and one check seed ([−0.0006, +0.0138] under 20260907), starred under the other ([+0.0003, +0.0143] under 20260906); on the three nearest cohorts it is +0.0083 [−0.0023, +0.0190] and holds zero; with one context draw held fixed it is starred on two draws, +0.0079 [+0.0027, +0.0125] and +0.0090 [+0.0045, +0.0137], and holds zero on the third, +0.0050 [−0.00003, +0.0093]. The star the one sign change between the protocols turns on holds in that pooling and on two of the three draws and in neither other cohort pooling, and the sign of the in-time difference is not established, its fold spread holding zero, so the pair is read as the criterion reads it and as no more than that. TabPFN − GBM is not starred out of time, +0.0036 [−0.0036, +0.0115], and holds zero in time, −0.0022 [−0.0214, +0.0176]. Criterion 2, that the scalar sees the level, does not fire under the script's rule, each order taken on the table's means at full precision; in time the Brier means of the five models lie within 0.00007 of one another and inside every fold range, so the in-time orderings are ties read as orderings, and under a tie-tolerant reading the criterion is undetermined in time and agrees out of time. Out of time the Brier score, the log-loss and the absolute log O/E order the five models alike, GBM, GBM-50k, scorecard, TabICL, TabPFN; in time the absolute log O/E puts GBM-50k first and both scalars put the GBM first, among three classical models whose levels sit inside one another's fold ranges, and both foundation models are last under five of the six orderings; under the in-time Brier score TabICL is ahead of GBM-50k by 0.000005, 0.022768 against 0.022773. What the scalar misses is the size and not the order: in time the foundation models' Brier score is 0.0228 against 0.0227 to 0.0228 for the classical models, while their observed over expected is 1.47 and 1.37 against 1.01 for each classical model; out of time it is 2.15 and 1.88 against 1.43 to 1.49. Criterion 3, that the threshold is unreadable, does not fire: the F1-optimal threshold puts 2.50% to 21.45% of a test cell above it on every fold and draw of the five models. The branch kill, that the choice of protocol does not matter, does not fire; its two legs separate. On Gini the in-time gap of TabICL − GBM lies outside the out-of-time interval. On level, the absolute log O/E against the GBM, TabICL's gap is the same under both protocols, +0.2755 in time with a fold spread of [+0.1849, +0.3420] and +0.2773 [+0.2442, +0.3007] out of time, each inside the other; TabPFN's is +0.3434 [+0.2680, +0.4106] in time against +0.4099 [+0.3778, +0.4322] out of time, the out-of-time gap inside the fold spread and the in-time gap below the interval. A random fold of the pool already shows the foundation models' level shortfall at the shipped temperature: the temperature sets it rather than the cohorts, and out of time the pool's rate adds to it. At 1.0 the in-time level of both is inside the span of the classical models' fold ranges, observed over expected 1.01 for TabPFN and 0.95 for TabICL, and the rows at 1.0 enter no verdict. F1 at the chosen threshold is measured as a mean with its range and with no paired interval: in time the GBM's mean is 0.1028 with a fold range of [0.0852, 0.1199], TabPFN's 0.1013 [0.0786, 0.1256] and TabICL's 0.0970 [0.0708, 0.1130]; out of time TabICL's is 0.1278 with a range over the fold thresholds of [0.1181, 0.1338], TabPFN's 0.1256 [0.0974, 0.1340] and the GBM's 0.1253 [0.1203, 0.1297]. The table, `experiments/2026-09-23-lc-2015h1e-protocols5-tfm`, is `scripts/protocol_table.py` over the two classical folds runs and the twenty foundation-model directories the table names: TabPFN at 0.9 and at 1.0 on the Apple node (`tabpfn` 8.5.0, checkpoint `tabpfn-v3-classifier-v3_default.ckpt`), TabICL at 0.9 on one rented RTX 4090 (`tabicl` 2.1.1, checkpoint `tabicl-classifier-v2-20260212.ckpt`, Python 3.12.14, torch 2.14.0, CUDA 13.0), and TabICL at 1.0 derived per fold in `experiments/2026-09-23-lc-2015h1e-fold1-tabicl-t1-derived` to `-fold5-`, each checked on 70,000 rows against that fold's own cells scored at 1.0 within 1.7 × 10⁻⁷ on the probability against a tolerance of 10⁻⁶; each fold is read against its own context, and an in-time entry is the mean over folds and context seeds with the spread across them in brackets; the table's out-of-time means are read from the `metrics.csv` of the pooling the pre-registration names, `experiments/2026-09-11-lc-2015h1e-intervals-grid`, which is byte-identical to the cited pooling's. An in-time bracket is the least and the greatest of five or fifteen unit values and an out-of-time interval is a bootstrap interval of a mean pooled over 220,000 rows; the two are of different kinds, and where the branch kill reads one against the other it reads them as registered. The out-of-time TabICL rows were scored on a hosted T4 and the in-time rows on the 4090; the difference between the two stacks on fold one is recorded in EXP-004's log entry of 2026-09-23 and is not read here. The criteria are read by `scripts/exp004_criteria.py` from the table's `cells.csv` and the pooling's `paired.csv`, with these definitions fixed in its docstring at c5a0742, after the table and the pooling had been recorded at 801f60a and before the criteria were read, so the rule was chosen with the in-time numbers on file: the five models are the scorecard, the GBM, GBM-50k, TabPFN and TabICL at the shipped setting, the rows at 1.0 read beside in the same way and entering no verdict; an in-time difference A − B is taken per unit — on each fold, per context draw when either model is drawn (a drawn model against another drawn model on the same draw since the foundation models and the control read the same 50,000 rows, an undrawn model against each draw of a drawn one), and on the fold alone when neither is — its point the mean over the units and its fold spread the least and the greatest unit value, the convention of the table's brackets; an out-of-time difference and its interval are the pooling's row for the pair on all cohorts under the primary bootstrap seed with no draw held fixed, and a pair is starred when that row excludes zero; the pre-registration says only that a pair is starred in the recorded pooling, and the pooling's other rows for a pair are reported beside the criterion's and enter no verdict; criterion 1 fires when on every starred Gini pair the in-time difference has the out-of-time sign and its fold spread excludes zero on that side; criterion 2 fires when the Brier score and the log-loss both order the five models as the absolute log O/E orders them both in time and out of time, each order taken on the table's means at full precision; criterion 3 fires when on any in-time unit of any of the five models the threshold puts fewer than one row in a hundred of the test cell above it, or none; the branch kill fires when on Gini and on the absolute log O/E alike, for both foundation models against the GBM, the in-time gap lies inside the out-of-time interval and the out-of-time gap lies inside the in-time fold spread. The pre-registration writes criterion 1 on AUC; the pooling's interval-bearing statistic is Gini, its monotone map, on which the pairs are read. The GBM's tuning budget is the study's grid per fold; the scorecard and the foundation models are tuned on nothing, the asymmetry EXP-002 names. The twenty-four-month label is C-004's, the same 2019-03-01 snapshot and five-month charge-off lag with the window twenty-four months, written beside the twelve-month label in each score file by the nine relabel runs `experiments/2026-09-24-lc-<build>-label24`, committed `b6e4ddc`. What is read was fixed in the EXP-002 log on 2026-09-24, in two entries, with the cells that enter and the rule for a disagreement, after every criterion row of the book was recorded and before any model's score was read against the twenty-four-month label. A scored cohort enters only if its twenty-four-month window has closed at the snapshot, which ends the trajectory at 2017Q1; a scored row whose window is open is dropped and never written as a non-default, a cell is read whole or not at all, and the criterion's floors apply unchanged. That leaves 64 cells over 15 cohorts on eight builds, 15 on 2013H1-E and two fewer on each later build, down to one on 2016H2-E, whose first scored cohort is 2017Q1; 2017H1-E contributes none, its first scored cohort being 2017Q3; 21,760,000 scored rows on 300,000 distinct loans. Each build's cells and its weight in each pooling are in `weights.json`; 2013H1-E carries 0.2344 of the cell-weighted mean. The twelve-month figures are the same 64 cells read with the twelve-month label from the same score copies, `experiments/2026-09-25-lc-arm-e-intervals-label24-at12`, the command differing in `--outcome` and the output directory alone; the pair was recorded a first time at `57f6bce` and not kept, and the `paired.csv` and `paired-seeds.csv` of that recording are byte-identical to these; the twelve-month figures are not the criterion's 99 cells of `experiments/2026-09-22-lc-arm-e-intervals`, on which C-007, C-008 and C-021 stand. Both runs pool as the criterion does: scope `arm`, cell-weighted, cohorts held fixed; cohort-blocked bootstrap, 200 resamples, seed 20260905, α = 0.05, repeated under the check seeds 20260906 (the first) and 20260907 (the second); the context draw, one of 20260911, 20260912 and 20260913, drawn with the resample; the cohorts-resampled pooling and each draw held fixed reported beside it. The two windows disagree on a pair when its difference lies beyond the interval on one and holds zero, or lies beyond it with the opposite sign, on the other, both read on the shared cells; a change of size with both intervals on the same side of zero, or both holding it, is not a disagreement. The pairs the criteria read are each foundation model against GBM-50k on the AUC slope on age with one intercept per build (H1) and with one per cohort (its second identification), against the full-pool scorecard on the mean absolute Cox-slope deviation (H2, as the amendment of 2026-09-04 rewrote it, with the control on identical rows beside), and against GBM-50k on PSI against the model's own training reference and against the build's first scored cohort (H3). A twelve-month probability read against a twenty-four-month outcome is off by the horizon, so the Cox slope against it is not a reading of calibration; observed over expected and the other level statistics are not read, nor is Gini. With one cell 2016H2-E has no slope of its own and no second stability reference, so the build-intercept slope and the other arm-wide rows read it as they read any build, while the rows that weight every build alike cannot be read with it; they are reported as unreadable, and no build is dropped to make them readable. The check reads nothing inside the hole and says nothing about it. | Freddie Mac single-family loan-level data as C-025's setting describes it: the vintage structure of EXP-003, the builds and cells of C-025 on the expanding arm and of C-018 on the rolling arm, with their models, the fourteen-column ratio matrix, the recorded GBM-50k as the criterion's control. The horizon check is registered in EXP-005's Setting, fixed before any model was fitted on this book: the twelve-month label on annual cohorts, read on the scores the primary label's builds produce, discrimination only, since a twenty-four-month probability ranks twelve-month outcomes and does not price them; it is reported and not tested and adds no criterion row. No recorded pooling read the twelve-month label before these runs; the note of 2026-09-17 lists the check as not yet computed. The open details were fixed in the EXP-005 log on 2026-09-24 and 2026-09-25, after every criterion row of the book was recorded, and every rule they fix reads the cells record and the build calendar alone and no score. The two runs here are the check's first recorded runs. The code ran unrecorded before them: on two expanding builds with two resamples and on the eight rolling builds with three resamples, both before the entry of 2026-09-25, and on the nine expanding builds with three resamples before the code was committed, and figures of these runs were opened. A point estimate does not depend on the number of resamples, so the points of both arms existed unrecorded before the recording, the rolling arm's before the entry of 2026-09-25. The three points of that entry were fixed after the first two of these runs, the nearest window among them after rows under the three-year window had been read, and each reads the cells record alone: the loans floor binds on no year, the nearest pooling keeps the criterion's three cohorts in the check's unit, and a mean over builds that includes an unreadable build withholds a number rather than choosing one. An annual cell is one build's scored rows of the two half-years of one calendar year taken together. One AUC is read on their union, with the bootstrap resampling inside it; a year a build does not score in both halves is no cell of that build; the cell's age is the mean of its halves' ages in quarters, which moves every cell of a build alike and leaves the within-build slope unchanged. A year enters when its annual cohort holds at least 5,000 labelled loans and 100 defaults under the twelve-month label on the book, the two floors of EXP-003, summed over the two halves from the cells record of `2026-09-13-fm-vintage-builds2`; an admitted year whose scored rows fall under either floor would stop the run, and none did. The years left out and their twelve-month defaults on the book are 2003 (82, a year only the expanding arm scores), 2013 (79), 2014 (76), 2015 (58), 2016 (52), 2018 (98), 2020 (85) and 2021 (71); the years admitted nearest the floor are 2019 at 101, 2009 at 102, 2010 at 104 and 2017 at 105, each read once and not with a second floor. The smallest annual cohort holds 47,189 labelled loans. The expanding arm has 64 cells over 13 annual cohorts on its nine builds, from 13 on 2002H2-E to 3 on 2018H2-E; the rolling arm has 51 cells over 12 annual cohorts on its eight builds. No build is dropped and no build is unreadable at the pooling of every cell. The nearest pooling takes each build's three youngest annual cohorts and then the floor; a build left with fewer than two cells there has no slope of its own, and the mean over builds is then reported as unreadable rather than taken over the builds that remain: on the expanding arm 2012H2-E, 2014H2-E and 2018H2-E, on the rolling arm 2012H2-R, 2014H2-R and 2018H2-R. What is read is H1 only: the slope of AUC on age in quarters with one intercept per build, the criterion's identification, and with one intercept per cohort beside it, at the criterion's poolings on both arms: every cell with the cohorts held fixed or resampled; the nearest pooling; the mean over builds with every build weighted alike; each build on its own; each context draw held fixed. H1's criterion on this book is read on the expanding arm, where C-025 records it; the rolling arm's rows are reported as C-018's are, beside it. The rows at 1.0 are not read, as the Setting reads H1 once: TabICL's derived rows equal its rows at 0.9 on every finite row, and TabPFN's scored rows differ from its rows at 0.9 by at most 0.000006 in point, with the same star under every seed at every pooling of its criterion row. The Cox slope and the level are not read, since the check does not price; the stability index reads no label; the nominal matrix is not read, since the check is not a verdict. The pre-flag and flagged scopes are not read: they belong to the criteria, kill criterion 4 reading them on a criterion row's sign, and the check enters none, the reason the nominal matrix is not read. The sensitivity reading has no twelve-month counterpart here: `outcome_horizon` is built from the primary reading only. That the scopes are left out was fixed in the code and its test at `7a85efc`, before the runs, and not in a dated entry. Models: scorecard, full-pool GBM, GBM-50k on the same 50,000 context rows the foundation models read, the second control refitted on 2026-09-17, TabPFN-3 and TabICLv2 at defaults, the seeded models on the context draws 20260911, 20260912 and 20260913, numbered first to third in that order. On the rolling arm the second control's rows equal the recorded control's exactly, in these runs as in the half-year recording. Cohort-blocked bootstrap, the block the annual cohort, one index over the union of its halves shared by every build that scores it; 200 resamples, seed 20260905, α = 0.05, repeated under the check seeds 20260906 (the first) and 20260907 (the second); the context draw drawn with the resample jointly across models and builds, and the same pooling with each draw held fixed reported beside it under the primary seed. As EXP-005 reads the seeds, a star under the primary seed that a check seed loses is holding zero at the margin, and a star under a check seed alone is not a star. Each interval is at α on its own with no multiplicity adjustment; of the 168 arm-level differences the seed check covers on the expanding arm, 26 are starred under the primary seed and 20 change their star with the seed; on the rolling arm 33 and 2. No pair of the criterion is among the rows either run marks as having its draws disagree. The Evidence run pools the nine `2026-09-1[56]-fm-*h2e-intervals-grid` recordings with the nine `2026-09-17-fm-*h2e-control-refit` recordings; `experiments/2026-09-25-fm-arm-r-intervals-horizon` pools the eight `2026-09-16-fm-*h2r-intervals-grid` with the eight `2026-09-17-fm-*h2r-control-refit`, the inputs of the criterion poolings. The half-year figures are those of `experiments/2026-09-21-fm-arm-e-intervals-refit-control` (C-025) and `experiments/2026-09-22-fm-arm-r-intervals-refit-control` (C-018), on the twenty-four-month label and half-year cohorts. | experiments/2026-09-25-fm-arm-e-intervals-horizon | active |

The attribution is that of Dorador, Hurlin and Pérignon, SSRN 7431058, §5.3, p. 18, stated there and not measured (its row in `docs/landscape/prior-art.md`). The reading is the smoothing reading fixed in EXP-005's Setting by its note of 2026-09-17 (`c3dfa23`), which adds the same statistic to the arm poolings of EXP-002 and names this book's reading the weaker one: per foundation model, the mean over the criterion cells of its signed Cox slope minus the full-pool scorecard's on the same cell, cohorts held fixed, the context draw drawn with the resample, 200 resamples under the primary seed 20260905 and the check seeds 20260906 and 20260907, with the model's own mean signed slope over the same cells read against one beside it. The attribution is supported for a model when, at 1.0, its paired difference lies above zero under every seed with a point estimate at or above the minimum effect of 0.05 on the paired difference, and its own mean slope lies above one beyond its interval; it is contradicted when the difference lies below zero, or the model's own slope lies below one beyond its interval; a difference whose interval holds zero, or a star with a point under the minimum effect, reads neither way; a star under the primary seed that a check seed loses holds zero at the margin, and a star under a check seed alone is not a star. The minimum effect was fixed in that note with the width of the second book's H4 interval at arm scope in view, the note naming it as one of the three scales that set the threshold, beside the 0.9 factor of the shipped temperature and the error of the derived rows; the note was written before any pooled signed slope existed on either book, with the per-cell signed slopes of both books on the record and Lending Club's per-cohort slopes on 2015H1-E quoted in EXP-002's log of 2026-09-06, which is why the note calls this book's arm a replication of a sign on that build; none of them is claimed unseen, nor was the pooled mean absolute Cox-slope deviation of both books at 1.0, which H2's poolings had printed (`experiments/2026-09-12-lc-arm-e-intervals`, `experiments/2026-09-16-fm-arm-e-intervals`). It names the book weaker also for its narrower prevalence range, one product over five years, and for holding no in-sample pooling. The reading is at 1.0 with 0.9 beside it. TabPFN's rows at 1.0 are scored; TabICL's are derived from its 0.9 scores by the exact scale, checked on 70,000 rows of each build's own cells, the largest absolute probability gap 1.6 × 10⁻⁷ over the nine builds. The own-slope half carries an interval under the primary seed alone, because `paired-seeds.csv` holds difference rows only. The reading is reported and not tested: it enters no H-criterion, adds no row to the Multiplicity count and changes no verdict of C-007. It reads the two foundation models this grid scores, out of time; the attribution was made of TabPFN-2.6 on stratified random folds, and the row is not a reading of that model on that split. It was first read in EXP-002's log of 2026-09-18 on `experiments/2026-09-17-lc-arm-e-intervals`, which this run supersedes in two links: `experiments/2026-09-19-lc-arm-e-intervals` reproduces it byte for byte, as EXP-002's log of 2026-09-19, later, records, and this run reproduces that one with no star moved, as the log of 2026-09-22 records; every figure that entry printed on the expanding arm is this run's. The scopes on which the Statement's reading breaks are rows of the Evidence run's `paired.csv`: with the cohorts resampled at lines 4454-4466, with every build weighted alike at 310-322, the nearest pooling at 2275-2282, the draws held fixed at 4683-4690, 4907-4914 and 5131-5138, the per-build rows over every cell at 511-518, 707-714, 903-910, 1099-1106, 1295-1302, 1491-1498, 1687-1694, 1883-1890 and 2079-2086, and 2015H1-E at the nearest pooling at 3479-3486; their seed checks are in `paired-seeds.csv` at lines 3345-3346, 6789-6790 and 10233-10234 (cohorts resampled), 237-238, 3681-3682 and 7125-7126 (builds alike), 1707-1708, 5151-5152 and 8595-8596 (nearest), 384-385, 531-532, 1119-1120, 1266-1267 and 1413-1414 under 20260905, 3828-3829, 3975-3976, 4563-4564, 4710-4711 and 4857-4858 under 20260906 and 7272-7273, 7419-7420, 8007-8008, 8154-8155 and 8301-8302 under 20260907 (per build), and 2610-2611, 6054-6055 and 9498-9499 (2015H1-E, nearest). The attribution is that of Dorador, Hurlin and Pérignon, SSRN 7431058, §5.3, p. 18, stated there and not measured (its row in `docs/landscape/prior-art.md`). The reading is the smoothing reading fixed in EXP-005's Setting by its note of 2026-09-17 (`c3dfa23`): per foundation model, the mean over the criterion cells of its signed Cox slope minus the full-pool scorecard's on the same cell, cohorts held fixed, the context draw drawn with the resample, 200 resamples under the primary seed 20260905 and the check seeds 20260906 and 20260907, with the model's own mean signed slope over the same cells read against one beside it. The attribution is supported for a model when, at 1.0 on the expanding arm, its paired difference lies above zero under every seed with a point estimate at or above the minimum effect of 0.05 on the paired difference, and its own mean slope lies above one beyond its interval; it is contradicted when the difference lies below zero, or the model's own slope lies below one beyond its interval; a difference whose interval holds zero, or a star with a point under the minimum effect, reads neither way; a star under the primary seed that a check seed loses holds zero at the margin, and a star under a check seed alone is not a star. The minimum effect was fixed in that note with this book's H4 interval at arm scope in view, the note naming its width as one of the three scales that set the threshold, beside the 0.9 factor of the shipped temperature and the error of the derived rows; the note was written before any pooled signed slope existed on either book, with the per-cell signed slopes of every build on the record and not claimed unseen, nor was the pooled mean absolute Cox-slope deviation of both books at 1.0, which H2's poolings had printed (`experiments/2026-09-12-lc-arm-e-intervals`, `experiments/2026-09-16-fm-arm-e-intervals`). The reading is at 1.0 with 0.9 beside it. On the ratio matrix TabPFN's rows at 1.0 are scored and TabICL's derived from its 0.9 scores by the exact scale, checked on the 121,595 rows of 2004H2-E with a largest absolute probability gap of 1.4 × 10⁻⁷ and carried to the other eight builds by the identity of the library version, the checkpoint and the source temperature. The own-slope half carries an interval under the primary seed alone, because `paired-seeds.csv` holds difference rows only. The reading is reported and not tested: it enters no H-criterion, adds no row to the Multiplicity count and changes no verdict of C-027. It reads the two foundation models this grid scores, out of time; the attribution was made of TabPFN-2.6 on stratified random folds, and the row is not a reading of that model on that split. The ratio matrix's reading was first made in EXP-005's log of 2026-09-18 on `experiments/2026-09-18-fm-arm-e-intervals`, which this run supersedes in two links, each with its tables byte-identical: `experiments/2026-09-19-fm-arm-e-intervals`, as EXP-005's log of 2026-09-19, later, records, and this run, as the log of 2026-09-22 records; every figure that entry printed on the expanding arm is this run's. The reading is made on the ratio matrix with the nominal matrix's reading beside it, as the `upb_nominal` trigger of 2026-09-14 requires of every verdict of this book, the ratio's being the criterion's; the nominal reading is recorded in `experiments/2026-09-23-fm-arm-e-intervals-upb-nominal`, the same pooling of the same builds' cells scored on the matrix that carries the nominal loan amount in place of the ratio to the conforming limit, under the same scopes, seeds and draws, and was read in no log before this row. On that matrix the grid scored nothing at 1.0, so both models' rows at 1.0 are derived: TabICL's by the exact scale, checked on the 121,595 rows of 2004H2-E with a largest absolute probability gap of 1.3 × 10⁻⁷; TabPFN's approximately, checked on the four cells of 2004H2-E the falsifying run scored at 1.0, the Cox-slope gap between a derived and a scored cell at worst 0.00035, read from the run's record of each derivation (`derived_rows`). | C-045 | On the expanding arm of the Lending Club grid, read on the rows each foundation model was conditioned on — the 50,000 context rows of each of its three draws on each of the nine builds, twenty-seven contexts a model — the mean probability at the shipped temperature of 0.9 is, in point, below three quarters of the realised rate on every context and below two thirds on one: it is 0.681 to 0.712 of the realised rate for TabPFN and 0.656 to 0.736 for TabICL, and TabICL's 0.656, on 2014H2-E under draw 20260911, is the one context below two thirds, the next lowest being 0.667, on the same build under draw 20260912, two thirds to three decimals and above it only in the fourth. Observed over expected, the reciprocal of that fraction, is 1.405 to 1.469 for TabPFN and 1.359 to 1.524 for TabICL, no context's bootstrap interval holds one, and the lowest lower bound of any context is 1.332 for TabPFN and 1.286 for TabICL. At 1.0, TabPFN scored and TabICL derived, every one of the twenty-seven contexts of each holds one: 0.987 to 1.019 for TabPFN, the bounds nearest one 0.967 and 1.038, and 0.958 to 1.048 for TabICL, the bounds nearest one 0.989 and 1.006. On the falsifying build 2015H1-E, whose three contexts realise 2.25%, 2.28% and 2.34%, TabPFN at 0.9 reads 1.460, 1.424 and 1.439 and TabICL 1.470, 1.440 and 1.415; at 1.0 the same contexts read 1.003, 0.988 and 0.991 and 1.010, 0.999 and 0.980. The known answer holds on every build: the scorecard and the full-pool GBM on their own pool, 50,609 to 1,108,732 rows at realised rates of 2.33% to 2.70%, and GBM-50k on each of its three context draws, forty-five fitted cells in all, read 0.994 to 1.000, every one inside its bootstrap interval. The level the entries of 2026-09-06 read as about two thirds on one build and one draw is, within the range above, the level of every context of the arm at the shipped setting, and the temperature alone removes it on the rows the model was shown. That is where the row stops: a level of one on the conditioning set is a statement about the conditioning set, and observed over expected on a cohort scored out of time is read in the arm's own rows and not here. No sentence here is about a model class. | Lending Club as C-006's setting describes it, the nine builds 2013H1-E to 2017H1-E of the expanding arm, each read in a recording of its own by `scripts/in_sample_level.py` — `experiments/2026-09-23-lc-2013h1e-in-sample`, `-2013h2e-`, `-2014h1e-`, `-2014h2e-`, `-2015h1e-`, `-2015h2e-`, `-2016h1e-`, `-2016h2e-` and `-2017h1e-in-sample` — over the five score directories the arm pooling of C-006 reads for that build: the classical scores, TabICL at 0.9 scored on a hosted T4, TabICL at 1.0 derived from those rows, TabPFN at 0.9 and at 1.0 scored on the Apple node. A cell is a model's own reference rows: the whole training pool for the scorecard and the full-pool GBM, the 50,000-row context draw for GBM-50k and the foundation models under seeds 20260911, 20260912 and 20260913; seventeen cells a build, 1,396,052 rows on 2015H1-E. Observed over expected is observed defaults over the sum of the model's probabilities on the cell's rows; beside it the Clopper–Pearson interval on the count and the percentile bootstrap interval over 200 resamples of the cell's rows at seed 20260905, α = 0.05, and 'holds one' is read on the bootstrap interval, the binomial giving the same verdict on every cell. The mean probability as a fraction of the realised rate is the cell's mean probability over its realised rate, the reciprocal of observed over expected; the recordings do not store it, and it is read on the point estimate alone, so two thirds and three quarters are read in point and the intervals against one. The known answer is that a model fitted by likelihood reads one on its own training rows, and the fitted cells are read as that check; the scorecard's Cox fit on its pool reads slope one and intercept zero within 0.0001 on every build. The Cox slope of each context cell is fitted beside its level and pooled per arm; its reading is one-sided, a slope above one being confounded with the pull toward a row's own label, and it enters no verdict here. H5 is not read: with one build a recording the script asks for no ranking of an arm's ends. Every recording at commit c5a0742, clean tree, exit 0, each pinning the score directories it read. Package versions and checkpoints, read from the score directories' `node.json`: TabPFN is `tabpfn` 8.5.0 with checkpoint `tabpfn-v3-classifier-v3_default.ckpt`, torch 2.14.0 on Apple Silicon (MPS), Python 3.12.14, `softmax_temperature` 0.9 and, in the second pass, 1.0, `average_before_softmax` False; TabICL is `tabicl` 2.1.1 with checkpoint `tabicl-classifier-v2-20260212.ckpt`, torch 2.11.0+cu128 on a Tesla T4, Python 3.13.15, `softmax_temperature` 0.9, `average_logits` True, eight estimators, its rows at 1.0 derived by `scripts/derive_temperature.py` from its rows at 0.9 by the scale its library applies to the logit. On each build the derivation is checked by row on one context draw, 20260911, against rows the library scored at 1.0. Those rows are the 50,000 context rows and one 20,000-row cohort cell, 70,000 rows a build, and the largest absolute probability gap over the nine builds is 1.6 × 10⁻⁷, as `experiments/2026-09-22-lc-arm-e-intervals/intervals.json` records under `derived_rows`. The rows of the other two draws are carried by the same library version at the same checkpoint from the same source temperature. Nine of the twenty-seven TabICL contexts at 1.0 are checked by row; eighteen are carried. C-014 to C-016 are the second book's preconditions, the counterpart of C-003 to
C-005 on the first: the axis, the label, and the reading that decides what the
label counts. All three are read from one descriptive run with no split, no
model and no seed, and each names the reduction run that asserted the file
layout and hashed the sample files, because the run cited here reads the
reduced files rather than the published ones. C-016 exists because the choice
it records is not a nuisance parameter on this book: it moves the level, it
moves the number of cohorts the book can be read on, and at twelve months it
moves which cohort reads as the worst.

C-006 to C-013 are the first model claims of the study, three per book: the
slope of AUC on model age, the Cox-slope deviation and the population
stability of the score, each paired cell by cell against a control that reads
the identical context rows. Each is read on one recorded pooling, and every
figure in a row comes from that pooling alone; no number crosses the two
books. Where a pre-registration names a second identification, a second
reference, a second weighting or a second temperature, the row carries both,
and where the two disagree it claims no more than the weaker. C-009 and C-011
cite the pooling that also holds the refitted control, so both controls are
read from one run; the recorded control is the criterion's.

C-012 is the fourth row on the second book and the one hypothesis here with a
known answer to check against: a model read on the rows it was fitted on. Its
statistic is the one the shipped temperature explains on its own, and the row
says so — the reading that would separate a scale from a prior is the reading at
1.0, and this run separates them for neither foundation model.

C-013 restates C-010 with one clause of its Setting corrected. C-010 said the
derivation of TabICL's rows at 1.0 was checked on 121,595 rows of each build's
own cells; the run's record of the derivation names one checked build, 2004H2-E,
on whose rows alone a derived row is compared with a scored one, and carries the
check to the other eight by the identity of the library version, the checkpoint
and the source temperature. The figures of the row are unchanged: the check is
the same check and the gap the same gap. On the first book the same clause is
right as written, each build there carrying its own check.

C-021 and C-022 restate C-006 and C-013 with one figure of each corrected, the
lower bound, with every build weighted alike, of TabICL − GBM-50k on the slope
of AUC on the first book and of TabICL − scorecard on the Cox-slope deviation
on the second. Each was written one unit off in
its last place: the recorded bounds are −0.000494626 and +0.031149950, which
round to −0.00049 and +0.0311. Nothing else in either row changes, and no
interval changes its side of zero.

C-025 to C-028 restate C-009, C-011, C-022 and C-012 with the nominal matrix's
reading beside each verdict, which the second book's pre-registration requires
of every verdict once the `upb_nominal` trigger of 2026-09-14 fired. The
criterion's reading is the ratio matrix's on every row, and no criterion's
verdict changes. What the nominal matrix changes is stated in each verdict's
sentence: on H3 it fires the kill for TabPFN; on H2 it fires the kill at 1.0
as well as at 0.9; on H1 it takes the star from TabICL's difference against
the control. On H5 it changes nothing. C-020 carried its nominal reading from
the start.

C-037 restates C-018 with two clauses of its Statement corrected and the
nominal matrix's reading added. C-018 said the cohort-intercept differences
against GBM-50k were starred under all three seeds with the cohorts resampled
as well as held fixed. With the cohorts resampled TabPFN's star is lost under
the first check seed, [−0.000022, +0.000595], so there it holds zero at the
margin; TabICL's keeps its star under all three. C-018 also said that under the
cohort intercept the order of the models' own slopes inverts. It changes in
point without reversing: TabICL goes from the steepest to the shallowest and
the scorecard from the shallowest to second. Every figure C-018 printed is
right as written. C-037 also carries the reading on the nominal matrix, which
C-025 to C-028 gave the expanding arm's rows and which the book's log set
beside C-018 on 2026-09-23: there TabICL − GBM-50k lies below zero beyond the
interval on the build intercept with the cohorts fixed and above it on the
cohort intercept, so the weaker claim C-018 drew, that neither model's
discrimination decays faster than the control's, is the ratio matrix's alone.
C-037 remains a reading beside the criterion and no verdict.

C-038 to C-042 restate C-008, C-017, C-019, C-020 and C-026 with every sentence
that generalises — a "no", "neither", "only", "every", "both" or "the one" —
scoped to the pooling, the weighting, the matrix and the handling of the
context draws it was read on, and each names, with the rows listed in its
Setting, the scopes of the same run on which its reading breaks. Two of the
five were wrong as written. C-019 said that no reading on the rolling arm puts
either foundation model's PSI below the control's under either reference; over
every pre-flag cell both models lie below it beyond the interval under all
three seeds, and so does each, over every cell of a build, on four of the eight
builds. C-026 said that with every build weighted alike both references keep
the sign of the arm's reading; TabPFN's row against the first scored cohort
changes sign, +0.0005 over every cell and −0.0007 with every build weighted
alike, holding zero both ways, and by the pre-registration's rule a difference
whose two poolings disagree in sign is no property of the arm. C-026 also named
2016H2-E as the build on which the sign reverses; on 2002H2-E, the build with
the most cells, TabPFN's reverses as well. Its nominal account said that
2016H2-E reverses for both models; TabPFN's nominal row lies above the control
over every cell, so its row on 2016H2-E keeps that sign, and C-026 did not name
2008H2-E, where TabPFN's nominal row reverses with a star as it does on
2012H2-E. The other three were right on the pooling their opening sentence
names and read as wider. On the three cohorts nearest each build's date both
foundation models move further than the control from the first scored cohort,
beyond the interval under all three seeds, on the Lending Club grid (C-038) and
on the expanding arm of the Freddie Mac grid on both matrices (C-042). On the
rolling arm, with every build weighted alike, on the flagged cohorts and on the
nominal matrix, TabICL's Cox-slope deviation exceeds the control's beyond the
interval as TabPFN's does, where C-017 named TabPFN alone (C-039). With a
context draw held fixed, pre-flag and flagged H4 rows exclude zero where C-020
called one row the only such row, per date TabICL's H4 row lies above both
controls on 2006H2, and the nominal matrix moves the arm and flagged rows
across zero but not the pre-flag ones; on the ratio matrix the first draw held
fixed puts TabPFN's arm, pre-flag and flagged rows below the control beyond the
interval, and on the nominal matrix the second and the third each put both
models' above it (C-041). No verdict read at the criterion's pooling with the
draws pooled changes, and every figure the five rows printed is right as
written.

C-043 and C-044 enter the smoothing reading EXP-005's Setting fixed on
2026-09-17, before any pooled signed Cox slope existed on either book: the
attribution of SSRN 7431058, that TabPFN's weaker calibration on credit comes
from probabilities smoothed toward the mean, read on each foundation model's
signed Cox slope against the full-pool scorecard's at temperature 1.0 on the
expanding arm, with a minimum effect of 0.05 fixed with the second book's H4
interval in view. It is a reading beside H2, reported and not tested; it enters
no criterion and supersedes no row. It was read on both books' expanding arms
in their logs of 2026-09-18, on recordings these rows' runs supersede, with
every figure of those arms unchanged; the Freddie Mac row adds the nominal
matrix's reading, which no log had made. Support needs both halves, a paired
difference above the minimum effect and the model's own slope above one; no row
of the three runs meets both at any scope. At the criterion's pooling the
own-slope half contradicts the attribution for TabICL on Lending Club and for
both models on Freddie Mac on both matrices, and holds one for TabPFN on
Lending Club, where the book does not resolve it. Each book is read on its own;
the sign and the star carry across, and no number does.

C-045 restates C-023 with one clause of its Statement corrected. C-023 said
the mean probability at the shipped temperature of 0.9 is between two thirds
and three quarters of the realised rate on every context of the Lending Club
expanding arm. Its own figures put TabICL's observed over expected at up to
1.524, on 2014H2-E under draw 20260911, a mean probability of 0.656 of the
realised rate, and that context is below two thirds. It is the only one: the
next, on the same build under draw 20260912, is 0.667, and no context of
either model reaches three quarters. C-045 gives the fraction as the range
read from the nine recordings, 0.681 to 0.712 for TabPFN and 0.656 to 0.736 for
TabICL, read in point, with the intervals read against one as before. Every
figure C-023 printed is right as written; beside that clause, one back-reference of the Statement changes and the Setting gains the sentence that defines the fraction.

C-002 restates C-001 on a rerun of the same script at the same seed whose
manifest also pins the source of the `psi-inference` package the simulation
imports; its `results.csv` and `environment.txt` are byte-identical to those of
C-001's run.

C-003 to C-005 are the vintage structure, the label and the label's sensitivity
of the first book, and they are the precondition for every model claim made on
it. Each supersedes no row. They restate readings first recorded on 2026-09-04
for C-003 and C-005, and for C-004's counts and rates on 2026-08-30 with the
intervals, the floor and the unrecognised count added on 2026-09-04, from runs
